## 2026-08-25 17:05 IST - M4 E4.3 (issue #33) + E4.4 (issue #34): latency levers, loop hardening, and a live incident (issue #50) found and fixed

- Started M4 per the owner's "proceed" after M3 closed. Understand pass confirmed all four M4 issue premises still held against current code. Owner chose "E4.3 + E4.4 first" pacing, pausing before E4.1 (model/provider/region -- needs GCP ADC credentials) and E4.2 (deploy discipline -- needs an owner-run redeploy), both real external-credential/infra dependencies outside what this session can complete alone.
- **E4.3, all 3 latency levers**: Lever 2 (tool surface <=12) was already satisfied by E3.1, confirmed not re-done. Lever 1: `run_assistant.py`'s shared `_prepare_turn` now prefetches `get_driver_operational_context` once at turn start and injects it as a SystemMessage before the first LLM call -- what `tools.py`'s own docstring already claimed (E3.1) but never actually did. Lever 3: real token-level SSE, not a cosmetic wrapper -- required extracting the ~450-line tool loop into shared pieces (`_prepare_turn`/`_execute_tool_round`/`_finalize_content`/`_persist_and_build_result`) so a new `stream_assistant_turn` generator and the existing blocking `run_assistant` share identical decision logic, differing only in `astream`+`AIMessageChunk`-accumulation vs. `ainvoke`. New additive `POST /api/v1/chat/stream`; `/chat`/`/chat/message` untouched per the issue's own rollback note. AgentCore passthrough streaming deferred (documented limitation, not silently unstreamed).
- **Independent, severe live bug found while refactoring the tool loop, filed as its own incident (issue #50) and fixed in the same pass**: `for turn in history:` silently shadowed `turn = TurnLatency()` (Python `for` targets aren't block-scoped) -- every driver thread's **second message onward** crashed with `AttributeError` the instant the LLM responded. Confirmed present in the just-pushed `38c4bd5`, invisible to every existing test (all used an empty `history` fixture). Fixed by renaming the loop variable; a dedicated regression test exercises non-empty history.
- **E4.4, all 3 sub-issues**: (1) `llm_call_timeout_seconds` (30s default) wired into both LLM client classes via their `timeout` kwarg (verified the `ChatOpenAI` alias to `request_timeout` genuinely works, not assumed); `turn_deadline_seconds` (45s default) bounds the whole turn via `asyncio.wait_for` in the blocking path and a between-round check in the streamed path (can't wrap an async generator's iteration in `wait_for`). (2) The DB session hold: added `db.session.release_transaction()` (commit falling back to rollback, never raising) wired into `get_execution_context` (every authenticated request), the prefetch, and after every tool call in `_execute_tool_round` -- shortening the hold, not raising `pool_size` (which would reproduce the 2026-08-17 `EMAXCONNSESSION` incident). Live-verified against production: `session.in_transaction()` True before, False after, session still fully usable. (3) Native Redis protocol: added `redis>=5.0.0` and an opt-in `upstash_redis_native_url` setting; `load_turn_context`/`append_turn` (the actual chat-turn hot path) branch to a real async `redis.asyncio` client when configured, falling back byte-identically to the existing REST client when not. Deliberately scoped to just those two methods -- the recommendation-staleness methods used from `allocation.py`/`eta_service.py`'s D1 booking path stay untouched, out of this epic's scope. Ships complete and unit-tested; live end-to-end verification needs the owner to supply the native credential.
- Verification: `python -m compileall app tests -q` clean throughout. Full backend unit suite grew from 361 (M3 baseline) to **385 passed, 0 failed** across this pass (8 new for E4.3, 16 new for E4.4/native-redis/session-hold). Every existing `ConversationMemory` caller and test fixture across the codebase updated for the new async method signatures, including a pre-existing dedicated test file this pass hadn't originally scoped to touch -- caught by the suite itself, not missed. `app.openapi()` confirms `/api/v1/chat/stream` registered, 69 total routes.
- Rollback note: revert `backend/app/assistant/{run_assistant,llm}.py`, `backend/app/api/v1/routers/chat.py`, `backend/app/core/{deps,settings}.py`, `backend/app/db/session.py`, `backend/app/services/redis_memory.py`, `backend/pyproject.toml`/`uv.lock` (drops the new `redis` dependency), and the new/modified test files. No schema/data changes; no live deploy happened this pass (AgentCore untouched).

## 2026-08-17 09:25 IST - Fixed an escalation wording regression and a live database connection-pool exhaustion incident

- **Wording regression**: `Escalate this no-slot case for SHP-D16-NOSLOT.` produced a nonsensical "Please confirm that you want to update the ETA for shipment SHP-D16-NOSLOT to None." Root cause: the 08:10 IST confirm-gate prompt text described escalate_exception as "a two-step write, like report_delay_or_update_eta" — that direct comparison caused the model to borrow ETA-flavored vocabulary when describing the escalation's `CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED` preview, which has no ETA field at all. Verified via a direct `escalation_queue` query that zero rows were written for `SHP-D16-NOSLOT` — the confirm-gate structurally held; this was a cosmetic wording bug only. Fixed `backend/app/assistant/prompts.py` to describe escalate_exception fully self-contained (no reference to the other tool), explicitly forbidding ETA/timestamp/appointment language in its responses, and requiring the reply to restate only shipment_id/escalation_type/reason.
- **Live connection-pool exhaustion**: subsequent testing of `cancel_appointment` then `escalate_exception` (this time on an explicit "escalate this please" request) both failed with generic "I encountered an issue... due to a system limitation" errors. Diagnosed live: `asyncpg.exceptions.InternalServerError: (EMAXCONNSESSION) max clients reached in session mode - max clients are limited to pool_size: 15` — confirmed by this agent being unable to obtain even a debug connection through the app's own `DATABASE_URL` after 5 retries over ~15s, i.e. a genuine, ongoing production outage on every DB write path.
  - Root cause: `backend/app/db/session.py`'s `create_async_engine(...)` never set explicit `pool_size`/`max_overflow`, so SQLAlchemy's unset defaults (`pool_size=5, max_overflow=10` = up to 15 connections from a *single process*) let one ECS task or AgentCore Runtime container alone exhaust Supavisor's *entire* project-wide session-mode connection budget (fixed at 15). This is a latent mismatch introduced by the 05:35 IST switch to session-mode pooling (needed to fix `DuplicatePreparedStatementError`) — transaction-mode pooling has very different, much more forgiving capacity characteristics, so the same default pool settings that were safe there became dangerous under session mode.
  - Fix: explicit `pool_size=3, max_overflow=2` (5 per process) in `session.py`, so multiple concurrent ECS tasks and AgentCore containers can actually share the 15-connection budget instead of one process claiming all of it.
  - Immediate relief (production was down for all DB writes at the time): connected via Supavisor's **transaction-mode** pooler port (6543) — a separate pool from the saturated session-mode one — to query `pg_stat_activity` directly. Found 16 backend connections proxied through Supavisor for the app's `postgres` role, 9 of them stuck `idle in transaction` holding nothing but a bare `BEGIN;` that was never committed or rolled back (a real leak, not just volume; the earliest predates this debugging session and lines up with the owner's live chat testing window, so most likely from real request traffic rather than the demo-reset script or any ad-hoc debug script — both were checked and correctly close their connections/engines in `finally` blocks). Terminated all 12 stuck/idle app-proxied backends via `pg_terminate_backend`; verified afterward that a normal session-mode connection (the app's actual `DATABASE_URL`) succeeds again, with only 7 baseline sessions remaining.
  - Added `docs/scripts/free_stuck_db_connections.py` (dry-run by default, `--confirm` to actually terminate) as a repeatable, self-serve version of this fix — connects via the transaction-mode port so it works even when session mode is fully saturated, only ever touches `usename='postgres' AND application_name='Supavisor'` backends (never Supabase's own internal services), and never its own connection. Wired into `docs/PRESENTATION_CHECKLIST.md` §7 Recovery table as a new symptom/fix row.
  - **Not fully root-caused**: why those 9 connections were left `idle in transaction` in the first place is still open. `backend/app/core/deps.py`'s `get_db_session` dependency (`async with db.session_factory() as session: yield session`) looks correct in isolation — SQLAlchemy's `AsyncSession.close()` on `__aexit__` should roll back and release even on an exception. The smaller `pool_size` caps future blast radius (a leaking process can now hold at most 5 connections, not 15) but does not prevent the leak from recurring; worth a deeper investigation after the demo if `free_stuck_db_connections.py` needs running again.
- Verification: backend units unaffected (no test currently exercises this code path directly); `python -m compileall app` PASS after both fixes. ECS redeployed twice more this turn (`default-setuhaul-api:10` for the wording fix, `:11` for the pool-size fix), both canary rollouts completed cleanly, `/health/live` 200 throughout. **AgentCore redeploy for the pool-size fix is still owed** (codezip re-staged both times).
- Agent/surface: Claude Code.

## 2026-08-17 08:10 IST - escalate_exception hardened to a two-step confirm write after prompt-only fix failed live twice

## 2026-08-17 05:35 IST - Upstash region migration + Redis batching + Postgres pooler fix, all live-verified

- **Upstash region migration**: owner created a new Upstash Redis database with primary region `us-east-1` (previous DB was `ap-south-1` Mumbai, ~600ms round-trip from the `us-east-1` AgentCore/ECS compute — confirmed via CloudWatch trace deltas showing ~190ms/call). New `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL`/`UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` saved to `.env`/`.env.local` and pushed to SSM via `docs/scripts/put_hosting_ssm.py`. Old Mumbai DB left in place (owner's call whether to delete).
- **Redis call-count batching** in `backend/app/services/redis_memory.py`: added `load_turn_context()` combining `load_history`+`load_summaries`+`load_session` into one pipelined Upstash round trip (`Redis.pipeline()`); `append_turn()`'s 5 writes (rpush/ltrim/expire/set×2) now pipelined into one round trip; `maybe_summarize_history()` accepts an optional `known_message_count` to skip its own `LLEN` call; `snapshot()` also uses the new pipelined helper. `backend/app/assistant/run_assistant.py` reuses one `load_turn_context()` fetch for both the duplicate-message check and context building instead of two separate `load_history()` calls. Cuts a plain turn from ~10 sequential Upstash round trips to ~2. Verified: 84 backend units pass (extended `tests/unit/test_redis_memory.py`'s `_FakeRedis` with pipeline support); live smoke test against the real new Upstash instance confirmed correct read/write behavior.
- **Postgres `DuplicatePreparedStatementError` — two attempts, second one is the real fix.** Discovered live via AgentCore CLI invoke after the async-entrypoint fix: `get_driver_operational_context` (and later `get_exception_status`) failed with `asyncpg.exceptions.DuplicatePreparedStatementError: prepared statement "__asyncpg_stmt_1__" already exists`, 100% reproducible on a fresh session's very first query.
  - First attempt: `poolclass=NullPool` on the SQLAlchemy engine (`backend/app/db/session.py`), theorizing SQLAlchemy's own pooling layered on Supabase's Supavisor transaction-mode pooler was the cause. Local stress test (15 concurrent independent sessions) showed zero errors, tests passed, deployed to ECS + AgentCore — but the live failure **persisted identically**, 3/3 reproductions.
  - Root cause, found by inspecting `pg_prepared_statements` directly on the pooled backend: Supavisor's **transaction-mode pooler (port `6543`)** can route a connection to a different physical Postgres backend between statements without resetting session state. asyncpg unconditionally names its handshake/query statements sequentially (`__asyncpg_stmt_1__`, `_2__`, ...) starting from 1 on every connection, so any two client connections that ever share a physical backend without a reset collide deterministically — independent of `statement_cache_size` or SQLAlchemy-level pooling. This is a documented asyncpg+PgBouncer-transaction-mode incompatibility, not something app-level connection config alone can fix.
  - Real fix: switched `DATABASE_URL` to Supavisor's **session-mode pooler (port `5432`)** on the same host — each pooled connection gets one stable dedicated backend for its lifetime, which is what makes prepared statements safe again. Reverted `NullPool` (no longer needed, was strictly worse for connection reuse); restored `pool_pre_ping=True`. Verified live via 35 total connect/query/close cycles (20 sequential + 15 concurrent, both through raw asyncpg and through the app's actual `Database` class) — zero errors, versus the prior 100% failure rate. New `database-url` SSM value pushed.
- **Deployment sequence** (all via ECS Express Mode canary rollout + `agentcore.cmd deploy --yes`, owner-run for AgentCore): three ECS redeploys (Redis batching, NullPool attempt, session-mode pooler fix) and two AgentCore redeploys landed this session. Live-verified post-deploy: `get_exception_status` and the `get_current_user_context`+`get_driver_operational_context` sequence (the two exact previously-failing scenarios) both succeed cleanly, repeatedly, via `agentcore.cmd invoke` against `driver.resched@setuhaul.com` / `DRV-RS-01` (isolated sandbox, cast untouched).
- Verification: 84 backend unit tests pass throughout (unaffected by infra-only changes). Live AgentCore Runtime invokes confirm both DB-tool failure modes are resolved. Redis latency improvement not yet measured via CloudWatch (code + region change deployed; a fresh trace comparison against the original 16.17s/25-event baseline is still owner's to check in the console).
- Agent/surface: Claude Code.

## 2026-08-17 04:10 IST - Fix AgentCore event-loop bug + escalation resolution-note gap

- **Root cause found and fixed** for the LangSmith `TOOL_ERROR` seen on `get_exception_status`, `get_facility_details`, and other DB-backed Driver tools on the hosted AgentCore Runtime: `Task ... got Future ... attached to a different loop`. `backend/app/assistant/agentcore_main.py`'s `invoke_agent` entrypoint was declared as a sync function wrapping `asyncio.run(_run_turn(body))`, so the Bedrock AgentCore SDK dispatched it via a plain threadpool and a brand-new event loop was created on every single invocation, while the process-level `db` singleton (`backend/app/db/session.py`) lazily created its asyncpg pool once and bound it to whichever loop happened to be running first. Every later invocation on the same warm container hit the stale pool on a mismatched loop; `pool_pre_ping` evicted the bad connection but didn't catch the raw asyncio `RuntimeError`, so the first tool call in that mismatch failed and a retry (fresh connection, current loop) succeeded — exactly the observed "fails once, works on re-ask" pattern.
- Fixed by converting `invoke_agent` to a native `async def` entrypoint (`return await _run_turn(body)` instead of `asyncio.run(...)`), so the SDK's own `bedrock_agentcore/runtime/app.py` `_ensure_worker_loop`/`_invoke_handler` machinery schedules every invocation on the one persistent worker loop it already maintains per warm container — the mechanism the SDK provides specifically to avoid this class of bug, previously unused because the handler wasn't `async`. Removed the now-unused `import asyncio`. No change to `_run_turn`, `_ensure_db`, or tool code. Side benefit: also removes a pre-existing thread-safety race on `if db.engine is None: db.configure(...)` in `_ensure_db()` under concurrent invocations, since all invocations now run on a single thread.
- **Second, separate gap found and fixed** while auditing Aman Kumar Goswami's `be62264` ("persist resolution metadata and sync driver exceptions on escalation resolve"), pulled from `origin/main` this session: `resolve_escalation()` correctly syncs `driver_exceptions.exception_status = 'RESOLVED'`, but the free-text `resolution_note` an Ops/Admin user enters in the "Mark Resolved" modal (`frontend/src/features/operator/OpsHomes.tsx`) was accepted by the API and silently discarded — no DB column existed for it, so a driver asking "what's my status" would see the new status but never the remark. Added additive migration `supabase/migrations/20260817040000_escalation_resolution_note.sql` (`resolution_note text NULL` on `public.escalation_queue` and `public.driver_exceptions`), threaded `resolution_note` through all three writes in `resolve_escalation()` (`backend/app/services/escalation_service.py`), and added it to the `get_exception_status` SELECT (`backend/app/services/driver_reads.py`) so the Driver LangChain tool now returns it.
- **Redeploy required for both fixes to take effect live** — neither is picked up automatically: (1) apply the new migration to the live Supabase DB; (2) rebuild/push the `setuhaul-api` Docker image to ECR and update the ECS Express Mode service, so the hosted Ops "Resolve" endpoint runs the current `escalation_service.py` (today's hosted backend predates even `be62264`, so live "Resolve" clicks are not yet persisting status/resolved_at/note at all); (3) redeploy the Bedrock AgentCore Runtime image/CodeZip so the hosted Driver chat picks up the `invoke_agent` async-entrypoint fix — bundle it with the driver_reads.py column addition in the same Runtime redeploy. Owner will perform the AWS redeploy.
- Verification: `PYTHONPATH=. pytest tests -q` from `backend/` — **84 passed, 3 skipped** (includes new `test_resolve_escalation_persists_resolution_note` and `test_get_exception_status_returns_resolution_note`). `python -c "from app.assistant import agentcore_main"` import smoke PASS. Migration not yet applied live; ECS/AgentCore redeploy not yet performed (owner deploying separately). No live re-test of the original failing scenario yet — pending redeploy.
- Agent/surface: Claude Code.

## 2026-08-16 21:30 IST - Isolated reschedule-demo driver + reschedule_appointment stale-hash bug fix

- User asked to give every shipment/driver an appointment so reschedule could be demoed. A read-only live sweep showed that is infeasible without violating feasibility/write-safety invariants: of 667 shipments, 563 are `COMPLETED`/`CANCELLED` (`SHIPMENT_NOT_ACTIVE`), and of the 104 eligible only 19 are bookable — 13 of those are demo-cast shipments (`SHP-D16-RAVI`, `RACE-A/B`, `CONTEND-01..10`) whose bulk-booking would break runbook Phases B/C/E/G. Agreed approach: one fully isolated sandbox driver instead.
- Added `supabase/demo/seed_reschedule_driver.py` + `rollback_reschedule_driver.py`. Creates driver `DRV-RS-01` / user `USR-RS-01` / four shipments (`SHP-RS-PENDING`, `SHP-RS-CONFIRMED`, `SHP-RS-OPEN`, `SHP-RS-NOSLOT`) at `FAC-GGN-01`, a facility the demo cast never touches. All appointment writes go through the production `request_slot` / `confirm_appointment` services (driver + admin `ExecutionContext`, not raw SQL), so the seed run doubles as a regression test of those services. `SHP-RS-NOSLOT` uses `HEAVY` dock type, which GGN has none of, guaranteeing a real `NO_SLOT` escalation.
- **Found and fixed a real bug while seeding**: `reschedule_appointment` (`backend/app/scheduling/allocation.py`) cancels the driver's old appointment, then calls the internal `request_slot(persist=False)` passing the *same* pre-cancel `displayed_recommendation_id`. That nested call re-validates against a freshly recomputed `find_feasible_slots`, which now includes the just-freed old slot (usually re-entering the top ranking) — so the hash never matches and every reschedule attempt failed with `SLOT_OPTIONS_STALE` on the first try, even with a genuinely fresh recommendation. Reproduced live 2/2 on both seeded shipments. No existing test caught it: the unit test mocks `find_feasible_slots` entirely, and no live integration test called `reschedule_appointment`. This affects the real driver-chat `reschedule_appointment` tool identically (same recommendation_id pass-through in `tools.py`), so it was a demo-blocking defect independent of this sandbox. Fix: the nested `request_slot` call no longer re-passes `displayed_recommendation_id`/`displayed_policy_version` — `reschedule_appointment` already validated both once, correctly, against the true pre-cancel snapshot, and already re-confirmed the new slot is a live feasible option immediately before cancelling; concurrency safety still comes from `request_slot`'s row lock and the DB unique constraints, not the hash compare.
- Appended optional Phase H to `docs/DEMO_MANUAL_RUNBOOK.md` (reschedule of a pending and a confirmed appointment, plus the guaranteed no-slot escalation); Phases A–G are unchanged. Documented the sandbox driver in `supabase/demo/README.md`. Noted there is currently no Ops UI confirm button for any appointment (confirm is REST/Swagger-only today, same as the existing Phase F note) — the seed script confirms via a script-local admin `ExecutionContext`, not any teammate's login.
- **Found and fixed a second, pre-existing bug**: `reset_demo_day.py --mode full` deleted `shipments WHERE shipment_id LIKE 'SHP-D16-%'` and `appointment_slots WHERE slot_id LIKE 'D16-%'` unconditionally, but `appointments.shipment_id` and `appointments.slot_id` are both `ON DELETE NO ACTION` — so any surviving non-`D16`-prefixed appointment (any live chat booking made during Phase B/C/G, or a Dispatch Console auto-booking) referencing one of those rows would abort the whole reset transaction with a foreign-key violation. Confirmed this already existed independent of the new sandbox: a live Dispatch Console booking (`APT-F16FD7C41E41` on `SHP-DISP-83C3F2C0`, slot `D16-SLT-02739`) currently in the DB would already trigger it. Fixed both `DELETE` statements in `supabase/demo/reset_demo_day.py` to skip any row still referenced by a surviving appointment; corrected the `--dry-run` preview counts to simulate the same same-transaction ordering (they were showing a misleading "574 kept" before the fix, "0 kept" after, matching a direct simulation query). `--mode cast` (the default, most-used path) was already unaffected and remains unaffected. `--mode full --confirm` was not executed live (rare/destructive, not requested) — verified via `--dry-run` only.
- Verification: dry-run then live seed on Supabase — 2× `SLOT_REQUESTED` + 1× `APPOINTMENT_CONFIRMED`; isolation checks confirmed zero writes at `FAC-JAI-01`, `D16-SLT-RACE` free, and all cast shipments still with no active appointment (total shipments 667→671, exactly +4). Reschedule smoke on both `SHP-RS-PENDING` and `SHP-RS-CONFIRMED` **PASS** after the fix (`SLOT_REQUESTED`, old appointment `CANCELLED` with `replaced_appointment_id` set, no double-claims); negative test with a bogus `recommendation_id` still correctly returns `SLOT_OPTIONS_STALE`, confirming genuine staleness detection is intact. Backend unit suite **81 passed**. Live integration `SETUHAUL_RUN_LIVE_DB_TESTS=1 pytest tests/integration/test_live_demo_day_load.py -q` **2 passed** (10×4 scarce load still 4 winners/6 conflicts/zero double-books; D16 cast smoke unaffected). - Added 5 new database service functions in `backend/app/services/driver_reads.py`:

  5. `get_dock_maintenance_alerts`: Queries active dock maintenance and outage events from `dock_status_events` and `docks`.

## 2026-08-22 17:49 IST - M0 implemented: live defects fixed, twelve-line latency work, six-metric baseline (uncommitted, pending owner review)

- Implemented and independently verified all of GitHub milestone M0 (issues #6-#15) via two parallel specialist-agent dispatches (fullstack-engineer for E0.1, latency-engineer for E0.2+E0.3), each restricted to disjoint files and instructed not to commit/push. Every change was re-verified directly against the actual diff and a real test run before being trusted, per this session's established discipline of not trusting an agent's closing summary alone.
- **E0.1 (#9, #10, #11)**: `report_vehicle_breakdown_or_incident` (backend/app/services/driver_reads.py) now commits — the missing `session.commit()` was why driver incident reports were silently discarded while the assistant replied "PERSISTED". `ExecutionContext.is_admin` (backend/app/core/execution_context.py) was the actual root cause of #10, not the three routers named in the issue: it conflated write-authority with global read-reach, so a new `has_global_read_scope` property was added and every real consumer was correctly split (execution_context.py, allocation.py, feasibility.py, escalation_service.py, health_auth.py, operations.py, shipments.py, driver_reads.py) — more thorough than the issue's original per-router framing, and verified correct. `POST /dispatch/shipments` (backend/app/api/v1/routers/dispatch.py, backend/app/services/dispatch_service.py) now requires `Idempotency-Key` and reuses the existing `services/idempotency.py` ledger instead of minting a random key per call; also fixed a second bug found along the way (a caller-supplied duplicate `shipment_id` previously surfaced as an unhandled 500, now a typed 409).
- **E0.2 (#12-#15)**: `aws_region` default changed to `ap-south-1` (backend/app/core/settings.py), with a new `assert_region_alignment()` called at both process entrypoints (backend/app/main.py lifespan, backend/app/assistant/agentcore_main.py) that fails startup loudly on a region mismatch, with a deliberate `ALLOW_REGION_MISMATCH` escape hatch and a separate CRITICAL-only path for a remote AgentCore ARN in the wrong region (can't be fixed by this process's own config). `provider.force_flush()` removed from the response path (backend/app/assistant/observability.py); a new `shutdown_telemetry()` runs at process shutdown instead, verified against the vendored SDK that `PeriodicExportingMetricReader`'s own export interval and its atexit handler already cover the interim. `JwtVerifier` is now process-scoped (backend/app/core/deps.py) via a module-level dict keyed on auth settings, not `@lru_cache` on `Settings` directly (verified `Settings` is unhashable under pydantic, so that would have raised on every request). `maybe_summarize_history` (backend/app/assistant/run_assistant.py) is now fire-and-forget via a new `_spawn_background()` helper that holds a strong task reference (asyncio only weak-references running tasks) and logs failures without surfacing them to the driver.
- **E0.3 (#8)**: instrumented all six `TECH_STACK.md` §10 measurements in backend/app/assistant/observability.py (TTFT, hop-count distribution, per-tool DB latency, LLM latency split, prompt-cache hit rate, Redis RTT), wired into run_assistant.py and redis_memory.py, all degrade-safe (no-op without an OTEL distro) and also attached to the LangSmith parent run since that is the only place they are readable outside AgentCore today. Two honest limitations recorded rather than faked: TTFT currently equals the whole turn (no streaming path exists yet — a separate, later epic), and the LLM network-vs-inference split is not observable with the installed provider SDKs (verified against both `langchain_openai` 0.3.35 and `langchain_google_genai` 2.1.12's actual `response_metadata` shape) — it is wired to activate the moment a streaming caller supplies a first-token timestamp, and until then is recorded honestly as `phase="total_no_stream"` rather than a fabricated split. `cache_hit_rate` returns `None`, not `0.0`, when no provider reports token counts, so an unmeasured cache is never confused with a cold one.
- **Real deploy consequence, not yet acted on**: the live ECS/AgentCore stacks have `AWS_REGION=us-east-1` baked directly into their deploy configs (`deploy/express-create.json`, `agentcore/agentcore.json` — confirmed by `COMPARISON-deployment.md`). Once this change is deployed, both will refuse to boot (`RegionMismatchError`) unless `ALLOW_REGION_MISMATCH=true` is set in their live config first, or the actual region migration (issue #45/E7.1) happens first. This is the intended loud failure, not a defect, but it is a real pre-deploy decision for the owner — flagged, not silently resolved.

## 2026-08-23 06:51 IST - E1.4 (issue #19) feasibility completion: Stage 0 multi-day horizon, facility-rule and driver-window hard constraints, redundant casts dropped

- Implemented GitHub issue #19 (M1, `risk:high`) in `backend/app/scheduling/feasibility.py`, `backend/app/scheduling/constraints.json`, and `backend/tests/unit/test_scheduling_feasibility.py`. `backend/app/scheduling/allocation.py` deliberately untouched — a parallel agent owned it for E1.3 this session.
- **UPIV re-verification corrected the issue's own premise, and this is the load-bearing finding.** #19 states the `CAST(slot_end_ts AS timestamptz)` blocks `ix_slots_facility_time` and that removing it is what unblocks `NFR-003`. `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` against the live database shows that stopped being true the moment E1.1 landed: with the columns now real `timestamptz` the cast is a no-op the planner strips, and the *pre-change* query already reported `Index Cond: ((facility_id = 'FAC-JAI-01') AND (slot_end_ts > '2026-08-16 13:00:00+00'))` via `Index Scan using ix_slots_facility_time`. So the index was already in use; the `NFR-003` unblock was delivered by E1.1, not by this edit. The cast removal is correct hygiene (a no-op cast that reads as load-bearing is a trap for the next reader) but must not be credited with a performance win it did not produce.
- **`NFR-003` is still not met, for a different reason, measured not assumed.** End-to-end `find_feasible_slots` is ~60 ms median (10 samples, `SHP-D16-RAVI` and `SHP-D16-NOSLOT`) against a 50 ms budget, while the candidate SQL itself is 0.9–1.5 ms (47 rows for a typical 48 h window; 1.5 ms / 384 rows for the busiest window at `FAC-JAI-01`). A bare `SELECT 1` on the same connection is 10.0 ms median, so the four sequential round trips this function makes are a ~40 ms floor and account for essentially the whole budget. This is exactly `COMPARISON-latency.md` F16's prescription (collapse the round trips, don't optimise the query) and it is **not** closed by this issue. Caveat stated honestly: measured from the dev laptop, so the absolute RTT is not the production RTT — the structural conclusion (round-trip-count-bound, not query-bound) holds regardless.
- **Stage 0 multi-day horizon** (`SOLUTION_DESIGN.md` §5 Stage 0): candidate search is now bounded to a rolling 48 h horizon from the effective ETA (`SEARCH_HORIZON_HOURS`, exposed as a `horizon_hours` parameter), `LIMIT` raised 200→500 because 200 truncated inside a single day at the busiest facility and would have made the next-day path unreachable. `FeasibleSlotsResult` gains `outcome` (`FEASIBLE` / `NO_SAME_DAY_SLOT` / `NO_FEASIBLE_SLOT`), `search_horizon_hours`, `search_horizon_end_ts`, `eta_local_date`, `same_day_option_count`; `FeasibleSlotOption` gains `slot_local_date` and `is_same_day`. **Escalation is now raised only for `NO_FEASIBLE_SLOT`** — `NO_SAME_DAY_SLOT` returns options and no escalation, per §5's own table and §7.4's `NO_FEASIBLE_SLOT` row.
- `slot_local_date` exists because the returned ISO timestamps carry a `+00:00` offset whose date component is not always the facility-local date (a `19:00:00+00:00` slot is the next day in `Asia/Kolkata`). §5 Stage 0 names a wrong-day booking as "the single most likely place for a real-world wrong-day booking"; the renderer must use this field, not the ISO string's date.
- **Facility-rule evaluation with time-bounded effectivity** and **driver-window enforcement, both ends** (§5 Stage 1's first, second and third named additions). Two new hard constraints in `constraints.json` (`facility_rule_compliance` → `FACILITY_RULE_VIOLATION`, `driver_acceptable_window` → `DRIVER_WINDOW_VIOLATION`; `hard_constraint_ids()` goes 8→10, which flows into every option's `checked_constraints`). Three `facility_rules.rule_type` values are enforced mechanically — `LAST_NEW_START_TIME` (RULE005, strictly-after reading, evaluated against the real unload start `feasible_start`, not `slot_start_ts`), `HEAVY_DOCK_REQUIRED_KG` (RULE004), `REEFER_DOCK_REQUIRED` (RULE003) — with `effective_from`/`effective_to` honoured and **rule absence treated as permission, never inherited across facilities** (the query is scoped to one `facility_id`, so `FAC-GGN-01` cannot pick up Jaipur's cutoff). An unrecognised `rule_type` is ignored rather than guessed at, because `rule_value` is free text and the operational meaning lives in the human-readable `description`.
- **Zero additional database round trips**, verified by counting `session.execute` calls in `find_feasible_slots` at `HEAD` and in the working tree (4 both times). The driver window rides along as two correlated `MAX`/`MIN` aggregates on the existing shipment statement; the facility rules ride along as a `json_agg` on the existing facility statement. Adding a fifth and sixth trip would have moved `NFR-003` in the wrong direction while nominally closing a Stage 1 gap.
- **Live behavioural delta against the demo cast, measured before and after** (read-only, no writes): `SHP-D16-RAVI`, `RACE-A`, `CONTEND-01`, `CONTEND-10`, `MULTI-B` unchanged (5 options each, same slots, same scores); `SHP-D16-RACE-B` 5→4 options — the dropped `15:00–15:30Z` interval would finish 20:55 IST against that driver's stated `latest_acceptable_ts` of 20:40 IST, so this is the new constraint working correctly, not a regression; `SHP-D16-NOSLOT`, `LATE`, `EARLY`, `FUTURE` still `NO_FEASIBLE_SLOT` with escalation. Quantified in advance from the live data: `HEAVY_DOCK_REQUIRED_KG` changes nothing today (every active >25,000 kg shipment already requires a HEAVY dock); `LAST_NEW_START_TIME` newly excludes the 53 `FAC-JAI-01` OPEN slots starting strictly after 21:00 local; the four 16:00-closing facilities have a 16:00 cutoff, so their rule is a no-op against facility hours; only 33 of 264 `driver_exceptions` rows are in a live status, so the driver-window blast radius is small.

(4) `prompts.py` has no `NO_SAME_DAY_SLOT` template yet; the field is on the tool payload (it flows through `model_dump()`), but the mandatory "nothing works today" sentence is §7.2b conversation-design work.

## 2026-08-23 07:38 IST - E1.5 (issue #20, partial) expiry sweeper, plus a live production break its own verification uncovered (issue #47, fixed and closed)

- **E1.5**: added the SS9.1 injectable clock (`backend/app/core/clock.py` — `Clock` protocol, `SystemClock`, `FrozenClock` which rejects a naive instant on construction), the sweeper (`backend/app/scheduling/expiry.py` — locking bounded scan, `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, expire-then-release-`dock_occupancy`-then-audit, commit per appointment), and an internal endpoint (`backend/app/api/v1/routers/internal.py`, `POST /internal/jobs/expiry-sweep`, token-gated). PENDING_CONFIRMATION expiry (D9's 15-minute TTL) now works and was proven live, read-only, against 3 real appointments already 6-19 days stale — confirming M8 was genuinely unmet until this landed.
- The SS7.5.1 race resolution is Postgres's own documented `SELECT FOR UPDATE` re-evaluation semantics (planner commits first -> predicate no longer matches -> sweeper no-ops; sweeper commits first -> planner's own lock returns the now-EXPIRED row -> `ALREADY_ACTIONED` naming the winner with zero extra queries) -- verified against the Postgres 13.2.1 docs, not assumed. `SKIP LOCKED` chosen over blocking because EventBridge API destinations have a 5-second client execution timeout; at 1-minute cadence against a 15-minute TTL, deferring a contested row to the next tick is free.
- **Not closed, real forks recorded on the issue rather than silently decided**: EventBridge Scheduler cannot invoke an HTTPS endpoint directly (needs a rule + API destination, an infra change out of this session's scope); M8's escalate leg (`PENDING_EXPIRED_UNACTIONED`) isn't in `escalation_queue`'s check constraint yet; the notify leg has no `notification_outbox` table (already tracked, `TASKS.md` 3.4); D2 HELD-hold expiry is structurally unimplementable until `dock_occupancy` gains `state`/`expires_at` columns -- reported `supported=False` with a tested reason string rather than faked; the sweeper's endpoint fails closed (503, `SWEEPER_ACTOR_UNCONFIGURED`) until a service-account user exists for its audit trail, rather than misattributing its actions to a human `ADMIN` id.
- **The live verification this milestone has run throughout the session finally caught something severe**: E1.5's own read-only production test (not a hypothetical) proved that E1.1's `text` -> `timestamptz` conversion broke every write path still binding a Python `.isoformat()` string into one of the six converted tables -- `asyncpg` rejects a `str` for a `timestamptz` parameter outright. Confirmed independently, twice (once by the E1.5 agent, once again directly by the orchestrating session against production), byte-identical error message both times. Since the migration landed at 06:26 IST today, this had been silently breaking every real write to `appointments`/`shipments`/`eta_updates` through both the currently-deployed backend and the current working tree.
- Opened issue #47 (`risk:high`, `type:defect`, `src:incident`, M1) the moment this was confirmed, then fixed and closed it the same session rather than letting it sit. Fix: `allocation.py`, `eta_service.py`, `dispatch_service.py` (6 files total with tests) now bind real `datetime` objects into the six converted tables' columns while leaving `escalation_queue`/`audit_logs`/`driver_exceptions` (still `text`) untouched -- the affected/unaffected split was independently re-derived from `information_schema.columns` rather than trusted from the issue body, and verified in both directions (a control group: confirmed the *unaffected* columns still reject a `datetime` bind, proving the fix didn't over-correct).
- **Two additional real bugs found during the fix, beyond the original bind-type scope**: `eta_service`'s `declared_eta_ts` and `dispatch_service`'s `original_eta_ts` were being bound as raw, unparsed request strings -- not just the wrong type, but no validation at all (a tz-less or malformed ETA string was previously accepted silently into a `text` column). Both now go through a shared `parse_eta_ts()`, refusing `INVALID_ETA`/422 before any write if the value isn't offset-aware timezone-safe. This is a stricter API contract than existed before, forced by the type change rather than discretionary, and is called out explicitly rather than shipped as a silent behavior change.
- Deliberately did not thread the SS9.1 injectable clock through this fix (used direct `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` instead) -- these are audit/update timestamps no branch reads back to make a decision, unlike the sweeper's TTL comparisons; threading `clock=` through seven service functions and their routers would have been a refactor, not the one logical fix this incident needed. Recorded as a deliberate choice, not an oversight, with the one exception (`request_slot`'s `booked_at`, which *is* a TTL anchor) flagged in a code comment for a future, separate decision.
